I feel like having it speak back in a human-like way allows it to talk through it's answer to get to a solution. If you had a basic prompt that asked for text output, and had no personality or human-like qualities, would it actually get the right response?

If you think about it, a general intelligence like a human should be expected to talk through its responses to complex questions like a human actually would in their head. Asking something very complicated and saying "print(x)" should be a question reserved for super intelligences, not GI.

I wonder if we already have general intelligence but we are treating them too much like computers, and not people, by not allowing them to think through their responses in a conversational format.

I write my character entries something like this:

\>Tags: Sir Kit, Kit  
Sir Kit is a knight of the Kingdom of Larion. He is a middle aged man. He has short black hair and is balding. He has brown eyes. He has a thickset body. He wears a suit of platemail and carries a sword and shield. Women find him incredibly attractive and he seduces every girl he meets.

Maybe it's not the most efficient way, but I find it works well for what you manage to fit in there.

Anonymous Thu 16 Jul 2020 05:50:39 [No.](https://gwern.net/doc/ai/nn/transformer/gpt/inner-monologue/2020-07-16-4chan-vg-aidungeon2thread-holoinnermonologue.html#299591951 "Link to this post")[299591951](https://gwern.net/doc/ai/nn/transformer/gpt/inner-monologue/2020-07-16-4chan-vg-aidungeon2thread-holoinnermonologue.html#q299591951 "Reply to this post") [Report](https://gwern.net/doc/ai/nn/transformer/gpt/inner-monologue/2020-07-16-4chan-vg-aidungeon2thread-holoinnermonologue.html#)

[\>>299591724](https://gwern.net/doc/ai/nn/transformer/gpt/inner-monologue/2020-07-16-4chan-vg-aidungeon2thread-holoinnermonologue.html#299591724)  
I use Story exclusively.  
The thing is that you have to be much more proactive if you do it, and very willing to fine-tune and play with the AI. So many people have expectations about what they'll get and then complain about having to /retry or other things like it.  
Considering sometimes the AI will just laugh at your "Do" commands I'd rather have better control.  
Also, Do/Say have never been able to make the AI give me near-paragraphs of perverted output after I've been playing with it for long enough. It won't do it right away, but by the end of my sessions(most recently Suikoden related) it would do things like add two sentences to make my input a paragraph, then add a response in speech, an action, and then another whole sentence of action after that.

\>Go to Earth  
\>Meet an Atlantian scientist named Zad who build a portal into literal nothingness  
\>He originally wanted to use the portal to escape Earth because it's a hellhole, but fucked up the portal  
\>Tell him I'll teleport him off the planet if he helps me use the void portal to destroy Earth  
\>He agrees immediately, and we suck Earth into the void  
\>Send him off to a paradise realm filled with ass and titties  
\>MISSION COMPRETE  
\>Get ready for 5 days of steamy god lovin' with dream sisters  
\>Get home and collapse in bed. Almost immediately there's a knock on the door.  
\>Open it, it's a group of advanced hyper-humans calling themselves the Blue-Cloaks  
\>They're descendants of humans who became trapped in space because I destroyed their planet  
\>They attack me with a god-killing weapon that instantly erases my existence, and I black out  
\>Expect a game over, but hear a voice calling my name  
\>It's Nightmare the tentacle bestgirl. She tells me she literally beat up Death, saved my life, and nursed me back to health over the course of eons  
\>Find out the Blue-Cloaks raided the TAB and killed all the gods  
But most unforgivable of all, they made my waifu cry. These fuckers are going to regret showing up in my session.

\>Generic isekai prompt  
\>Spawn in random medieval village in the Kingdom of Oren  
\>MC is completely baffled and doesn't know what's going on in this medieval world  
\>Dude with spiky blond hair comes up to me and introduces himself as Kyle  
\>Offers me stay at his parents' inn, free of charge  
\>Have no real choice considering my predicament, so I follow  
\>When we get to the inn, I tell him about my isekai predicament  
\>He doesn't react  
\>I point this out and he says that he is experienced with this kind of thing  
\>Turns out Kyle is part of an interdimensional threat elimination squad  
\>He says that most of the things that are shunted through dimensions are evil creatures, not benign humans like me  
\>He goes on to say this

Tested the fairy's lifting capacity by making her carry larger and larger stacks of LEGO.  
She's about six inches tall and can lift and carry up to 300 grams (about the weight of three limes). I sort of wish she was a bit weaker but that's the limit we reached so I'm keeping it canon.  
Lately I've had to edit and undo more often, mainly because the game tends to forget she's too small to build LEGO towers without flying (something I disallowed on threat of hole-punching her wings). Other than that, she also seems to forget her lisp on occassion now but remembers after an edit or two.  
Here's the first snippet I've been able to make in a while that had absolutely no edits or undos. For context, she's been sleeping in an empty pickle jar for most of a week now and I promised her some sort of soft bedding if she cooperated during these tests.

Should I be editing the repeat lines out? Is it harmful for the AI's memory?  
Also what other tests can I do? I currently know the following:  
\>pushing/pulling weight capacity: 300g  
\>carrying weight capacity: 300g  
\>can eat/drink: yes  
\>needs to eat/drink: no  
\>excretes waste: no  
\>rate of matter removal: seemingly instant (no feeling of fullness, no swelling even after drinking a lot of water for her size)  
\>can breathe: yes  
\>neess to breathe: yes

Fairy update.  
I was giving her a few days to heal. We were watching TV in the living room when a man in a cheap suit knocked on the door. He said his name was Zalman and that he worked for Dreamwave, a company that watches fairies.  
He asked me for my fairy in exchange for $200,000 and a trip to the Caribbean. I said no and shut the door on him.

Continuing off my Cosmic Imp story from last night:  
\>The Blue-Cloaks, a group of vengeful space-faring humans, have killed off all the gods with a mysterious blue light capable of deleting even divine beings from existence  
\>Not even Jesus, and the Big Man Himself stood a chance  
\>Nightmare, the bestgirl eldritch goddess of dark dreams, and I are the only cosmic entities left  
\>We go to the HQ of the Council of the Gods to find it in ruins  
\>Discovered a time device that can only go back to a specific point in time: 2032 AD on Earth  
\>Apparently Earth is fucked in that year due to the beginnings of a cataclysmic event, humankind live on artificial islands scattered around the ocean and rely on some MacGuffin mineral as an energy source  
\>The energy extraction process produces a shitton of radioactive waste that is systematically dumped into the ocean, further poisoning the already ravaged Earth  
\>Find out 2032 AD is also the year the Blue-Cloaks first began their forays into space travel  
\>Now I'm on a mission to find the Blue-Cloaks and either prevent them from ever traveling to space, or somehow convincing them not to kill all the gods 10,000 years later  
\>To add salt to the wound, I'd be reincarnated as a human if I were to go back, because my god body wasn't conceived back then  
The worst part is I had to say goodbye to Nightmare, because the time travel device can only bring one entity back in time. However, I assured her that if I succeed, then the timeline will be reset, and we'll be back together before she knows it. From her perspective, it would only be an instant. I will do this to protect her thousand-toothed smile.

Dragon seems about equal to the full-gpt2 model (based on my casual derping around with both), which is 7GB to download and needs 12GB memory to run. It takes about 60 seconds per /retry on an average PC unless you have 12GB graphics card (a few hundred dollars) of the right type which gets you AI-dungeon times of a few seconds.

I don't think you can hire a machine capable of running GPT-3 off the cloud, so it's unlikely Dragon is actually GPT-3. I don't trust AI-dungeon because they're hiding what they're actually giving you for the money, but it's cheaper and easier than setting it up myself, at least for now.
